node_modules/.pnpm/@modelcontextprotocol+client@2.3.1/node_modules/@modelcontextprotocol/client/dist/src-WCy6ifGf.mjs\n\t/**\n\t* Cross-bundle `instanceof` support for the SDK error classes.\n\t*\n\t* `@modelcontextprotocol/client` and `@modelcontextprotocol/server` each bundle their\n\t* own copy of `core-internal`, so an error constructed by one package fails a\n\t* prototype-identity `instanceof` against the same class re-exported by the other —\n\t* exactly the check a dual-role process (gateway, host, in-process test) writes.\n\t*\n\t* Instead of prototype identity, branded classes stamp every instance with the brand\n\t* strings of its class chain under a registry symbol (`Symbol.for`, shared across\n\t* bundles and realms), and resolve `instanceof` via `Symbol.hasInstance` against the\n\t* brand set. Ordinary prototype-based `instanceof` is kept as a fallback so behavior\n\t* is unchanged for anything unbranded.\n\t*\n\t* A class participates by defining an **own** `mcpBrand` static (via a `static {}`\n\t* block, so nothing reaches the declaration files — a declared `protected static`\n\t* field would make the constructor types nominally incompatible across the bundled\n\t* copies) and (for hierarchy roots) installing {@linkcode brandedHasInstance} as\n\t* `Symbol.hasInstance`. User-defined subclasses that do not declare their own brand\n\t* keep plain prototype semantics — a foreign base-class instance never satisfies\n\t* `instanceof UserSubclass`.\n\t*\n\t* Prior art — the same stamp-and-hook shape ships at scale elsewhere: Node core\n\t* (stream.Writable since 2017, Console via a marker symbol, diagnostics_channel),\n\t* undici's whole error hierarchy (vendored into Node as fetch), googleapis/gaxios\n\t* (GaxiosError, Symbol.for marker), AWS SDK v3's ServiceException (which pairs a\n\t* Symbol.hasInstance override with a static isInstance guard, as we do), and zod v4\n\t* (Symbol.hasInstance on every schema class for cross-version interop).\n\t*\n\t* Contract notes:\n\t* - Participation criterion: **every error class exported from a public package that\n\t*   callers are documented to `instanceof` must be branded.** The per-package\n\t*   errorBrandConformance tests walk the export surfaces and fail naming any\n\t*   exported Error subclass that has not opted in.\n\t* - Brands assert **identity, not shape**: brand strings are version-less, so an\n\t*   instance from one SDK version matches the class of another. Members added to a\n\t*   branded class in a later version may be absent on a matched instance — read\n\t*   fields defensively, and treat branded classes as additive-only. The escape\n\t*   hatch when a release must break a branded class's read contract: change that\n\t*   class's brand string in the same release, which cleanly severs cross-version\n\t*   matching for that class. The per-package brand pins make the rename\n\t*   deliberate: errorSurfacePins.test.ts owns the core-internal brands, and each\n\t*   package's errorBrandConformance test pins its package-local ones.\n\t* - Cross-bundle matching requires **both** copies to be at or after the release\n\t*   that introduced branding; against an older copy, behavior degrades to plain\n\t*   prototype `instanceof` in both directions.\n\t* - A consumer re-bundling the SDK with property mangling (`mangle.props`) would\n\t*   break the brand statics; default esbuild/webpack/terser settings do not.\n\t*/\n\t/** Registry symbol — identical across bundled copies and realms. */\n\tconst BRANDS = Symbol.for(\"mcp.sdk.errorBrands\");\n\t/**\n\t* Stamp `instance` with the brand of every class in `ctor`'s chain that declares an\n\t* own `mcpBrand`. Call once from the hierarchy root's constructor with `new.target` —\n\t* subclasses inherit the stamping without touching their constructors.\n\t*\n\t* Constructor-time only: never stamp arbitrary objects. A stamped non-instance would\n\t* satisfy `instanceof` while lacking the prototype members (getters like `.status`)\n\t* that callers reach for after the check.\n\t*/\n\tfunction stampErrorBrands(instance, ctor) {\n\t\tconst brands = /* @__PURE__ */ new Set();\n\t\tlet current = ctor;\n\t\twhile (typeof current === \"function\") {\n\t\t\tconst brand = current.mcpBrand;\n\t\t\tif (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(current, \"mcpBrand\") && typeof brand === \"string\") brands.add(brand);\n\t\t\tcurrent = Object.getPrototypeOf(current);\n\t\t}\n\t\tif (brands.size === 0) return;\n\t\tObject.defineProperty(instance, BRANDS, {\n\t\t\tvalue: brands,\n\t\t\tenumerable: false,\n\t\t\tconfigurable: true\n\t\t});\n\t}\n\t/**\n\t* `Symbol.hasInstance` implementation for branded hierarchy roots. Matches when the\n\t* value carries the **own** brand of the class being tested against (cross-bundle\n\t* path), falling back to ordinary prototype-based `instanceof` otherwise.\n\t*/\n\tfunction brandedHasInstance(cls, value) {\n\t\ttry {\n\t\t\tif (typeof value === \"object\" && value !== null && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(cls, \"mcpBrand\") && typeof cls.mcpBrand === \"string\" && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(value, BRANDS)) {\n\t\t\t\tconst carried = value[BRANDS];\n\t\t\t\tif (carried && typeof carried.has === \"function\" && carried.has(cls.mcpBrand)) return true;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t} catch {}\n\t\treturn Function.prototype[Symbol.hasInstance].call(cls, value);\n\t}\n\t(class OAuthError extends Error {\n\t\tstatic {\n\t\t\tObject.defineProperty(this, \"mcpBrand\", { value: \"mcp.OAuthError\" });\n\t\t}\n\t\tstatic [Symbol.hasInstance](value) {\n\t\t\treturn brandedHasInstance(this, value);\n\t\t}\n\t\t/**\n\t\t* Brand-based type guard: equivalent to `value instanceof this`, as an\n\t\t* explicit static predicate (the axios/AWS-SDK `isInstance` style). Reads\n\t\t* the caller's own brand via `this`, so every branded subclass gets a\n\t\t* correctly-scoped guard by inheritance. Must be invoked on the class —\n\t\t* in callback position write `v => SdkError.isInstance(v)`, not\n\t\t* `.filter(SdkError.isInstance)` (detached calls throw rather than\n\t\t* silently matching nothing).\n\t\t*/\n\t\tstatic isInstance(value) {\n\t\t\tif (typeof this !== \"function\") throw new TypeError(\"isInstance must be called on the class (e.g. `SdkError.isInstance(value)`); The wire code is `-32602` (Invalid Params) on every protocol\n\t* revision — the spec MUST for revision 2026-07-28, and the value the v1.x\n\t* SDK has always emitted on earlier revisions. The error data echoes the\n\t* requested URI.\n\t*\n\t* Recognise this error by checking `error.data` is exactly `{ uri: string }`\n\t* (a `-32602` whose data carries `uri` and nothing else is resource-not-found;\n\t* any other `-32602` is an ordinary Invalid Params). src/artifactview.ts\n\t/**\n\t* Pure view model for the artifact (Artifact) semantic card view.\n\t*\n\t* The panel's artifact viewer organizes a YAML artifact by its schema fields\n\t* instead of dumping the file: brief, outline and claims get the three named\n\t* layouts the roadmap details call for, source.yaml gets the metadata layout\n\t* the sources view reuses, and every other artifact === \"draft\") return unit.draft;\n\t\treturn unit.artifacts[name] ?? null;\n\t}\n\t/** The registered sources (Source) in snapshot order. */\n\tfunction sourceEntriesOf(body) {\n\t\treturn body.snapshot?.sources ?? [];\n\t}\n\t// src/markdownview.ts\n\t/** Safe indexed access: out-of-range reads as the empty string. */\n\tfunction at(lines, index) {\n\t\treturn lines[index] ?? \"\";\n\t}\n\t/** Split a fenced-code line into its language and content. */\n\tfunction fenceOf(line) {\n\t\tconst match = line.match(/^(\\s*)(`{3,}|~{3,})(.*)$/);\n\t\tif (match === null) return null;\n\t\treturn {\n\t\t\tfence: (match[1] ?? \"\") + (match[2] ?? \"\"),\n\t\t\tlang: (match[3] ?? \"\").trim()\n\t\t};\n\t}\n\t/** Split one table row into cells (leading/trailing pipes dropped). */\n\tfunction tableCells(line) {\n\t\treturn line.replace(/^\\s*\\|/, \"\").replace(/\\|\\s*$/, \"\").split(\"|\").map((cell) => cell.trim());\n\t}\n\t/** Whether a line is the `| --- | --- |` separator under a table header. */\n\tfunction isTableSeparator(line) {\n\t\treturn /^\\s*\\|?[\\s:|-]+\\|?\\s*$/.test(line) && line.includes(\"-\") && line.includes(\"|\");\n\t}\n\t/**\n\t* Parse Markdown text into reading-view blocks. Line-based and total: every\n\t* input line lands in some block, unknown constructs become paragraphs.\n\t*/\n\tfunction renderMarkdown(text) {\n\t\tconst blocks = [];\n\t\tconst lines = text.split(\"\\n\");\n\t\tlet index = 0;\n\t\twhile (index < lines.length) {\n\t\t\tconst line = at(lines, index);\n\t\t\tconst fence = fenceOf(line);\n\t\t\tif (fence !== null) {\n\t\t\t\tconst body = [];\n\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t\twhile (index < lines.length && fenceOf(at(lines, index)) === null) {\n\t\t\t\t\tbody.push(at(lines, index));\n\t\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tif (index < lines.length) index += 1;\n\t\t\t\tblocks.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttype: \"code\",\n\t\t\t\t\tlang: fence.lang === \"\" ? null : fence.lang,\n\t\t\t\t\ttext: body.join(\"\\n\")\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif (line.trim() === \"\") {\n\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tconst heading = line.match(/^(#{1,6})\\s+(.*)$/);\n\t\t\tif (heading !== null) {\n\t\t\t\tblocks.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttype: \"heading\",\n\t\t\t\t\tlevel: (heading[1] ?? \"\").length,\n\t\t\t\t\ttext: (heading[2] ?? \"\").trim()\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif (/^\\s*>/.test(line)) {\n\t\t\t\tconst quoted = [];\n\t\t\t\twhile (index < lines.length && /^\\s*>/.test(at(lines, index))) {\n\t\t\t\t\tquoted.push(at(lines, index).replace(/^\\s*>\\s?/, \"\"));\n\t\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tblocks.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttype: \"quote\",\n\t\t\t\t\tblocks: renderMarkdown(quoted.join(\"\\n\"))\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tconst list = line.match(/^\\s*([-*+]|\\d+[.)])\\s+/);\n\t\t\tif (list !== null) {\n\t\t\t\tconst ordered = /\\d/.test(list[1] ?? \"\");\n\t\t\t\tconst items = [];\n\t\t\t\tconst sameKind = (marker) => /\\d/.test(marker) === ordered;\n\t\t\t\twhile (index < lines.length) {\n\t\t\t\t\tconst item = at(lines, index).match(/^\\s*([-*+]|\\d+[.)])\\s+(.*)$/);\n\t\t\t\t\tif (item === null || !sameKind(item[1] ?? \"\")) break;\n\t\t\t\t\titems.push(item[2] ?? \"\");\n\t\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tblocks.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttype: \"list\",\n\t\t\t\t\tordered,\n\t\t\t\t\titems\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif (line.includes(\"|\") && index + 1 < lines.length && isTableSeparator(at(lines, index + 1))) {\n\t\t\t\tconst header = tableCells(line);\n\t\t\t\tindex += 2;\n\t\t\t\tconst rows = [];\n\t\t\t\twhile (index < lines.length && at(lines, index).includes(\"|\") && at(lines, index).trim() !== \"\") {\n\t\t\t\t\trows.push(tableCells(at(lines, index)));\n\t\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tblocks.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttype: \"table\",\n\t\t\t\t\theader,\n\t\t\t\t\trows\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tconst paragraph = [];\n\t\t\twhile (index < lines.length && at(lines, index).trim() !== \"\" && fenceOf(at(lines, index)) === null && !/^(#{1,6})\\s+/.test(at(lines, index)) && !/^\\s*>/.test(at(lines, index)) && !/^\\s*([-*+]|\\d+[.)])\\s+/.test(at(lines, index))) {\n\t\t\t\tparagraph.push(at(lines, index));\n\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif (paragraph.length === 0) {\n\t\t\t\tparagraph.push(at(lines, index));\n\t\t\t\tindex += 1;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tblocks.push({\n\t\t\t\ttype: \"paragraph\",\n\t\t\t\ttext: paragraph.join(\"\\n\")\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn blocks;\n\t}\n\t/**\n\t* Color the structural parts of one Markdown source line: quote and list\n\t* markers, headings, fenced code, inline code spans and emphasis runs.\n\t* Joining the tokens reproduces the line exactly.\n\t*/\n\tfunction highlightMarkdownLine(line, inFence) {\n\t\tif (inFence) return { tokens: [{\n\t\t\ttext: line,\n\t\t\tcls: \"md-code\"\n\t\t}] };\n\t\tif (fenceOf(line) !== null) return { tokens: [{\n\t\t\ttext: line,\n\t\t\tcls: \"md-code\"\n\t\t}] };\n\t\tif (/^#{1,6}\\s/.test(line)) return { tokens: [{\n\t\t\ttext: line,\n\t\t\tcls: \"md-heading\"\n\t\t}] };\n\t\tif (/^\\s*>/.test(line)) return { tokens: [{\n\t\t\ttext: line,\n\t\t\tcls: \"md-quote\"\n\t\t}] };\n\t\tconst list = line.match(/^(\\s*)([-*+]|\\d+[.)])(\\s+)([\\s\\S]*)$/);\n\t\tif (list !== null) return { tokens: [...[\n\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\ttext: list[1] ?? \"\",\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"plain\"\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\ttext: list[2] ?? \"\",\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"md-list\"\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\ttext: list[3] ?? \"\",\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"plain\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t], ...inlineTokens(list[4] ?? \"\")] };\n\t\tif (line.includes(\"|\") && (line.trim().startsWith(\"|\") || line.trim().endsWith(\"|\"))) return { tokens: [{\n\t\t\ttext: line,\n\t\t\tcls: \"md-table\"\n\t\t}] };\n\t\treturn { tokens: inlineTokens(line) };\n\t}\n\t/** Highlight inline code spans and emphasis runs in plain text. */\n\tfunction inlineTokens(text) {\n\t\tconst tokens = [];\n\t\tlet rest = text;\n\t\twhile (rest !== \"\") {\n\t\t\tconst start = rest.search(/[`*]/);\n\t\t\tif (start < 0) {\n\t\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttext: rest,\n\t\t\t\t\tcls: \"plain\"\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\tbreak;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif (start > 0) tokens.push({\n\t\t\t\ttext: rest.slice(0, start),\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"plain\"\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t\tconst marker = rest[start];\n\t\t\tconst paired = marker === \"`\" ? rest.indexOf(\"`\", start + 1) : rest.indexOf(\"**\", start + 1);\n\t\t\tconst end = marker === \"`\" ? paired : paired >= 0 ? paired + 1 : -1;\n\t\t\tif (end < 0) {\n\t\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttext: rest.slice(start),\n\t\t\t\t\tcls: \"plain\"\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\tbreak;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\ttext: rest.slice(start, end + 1),\n\t\t\t\tcls: marker === \"`\" ? \"md-inline-code\" : \"md-emphasis\"\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t\trest = rest.slice(end + 1);\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn tokens;\n\t}\n\t/** Highlight whole Markdown source; tracks fence state across lines. */\n\tfunction highlightMarkdownSource(text) {\n\t\tconst out = [];\n\t\tlet inFence = false;\n\t\tfor (const line of text.split(\"\\n\")) {\n\t\t\tif (fenceOf(line) !== null) {\n\t\t\t\tout.push({ tokens: [{\n\t\t\t\t\ttext: line,\n\t\t\t\t\tcls: \"md-code\"\n\t\t\t\t}] });\n\t\t\t\tinFence = !inFence;\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tout.push(highlightMarkdownLine(line, inFence));\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn out;\n\t}\n\t// src/yamlhighlight.ts\n\t/** Split a scalar into anchor/alias/quoted parts. */\n\tfunction scalarTokens(raw) {\n\t\tconst tokens = [];\n\t\tlet rest = raw;\n\t\twhile (rest !== \"\") {\n\t\t\tconst bind = rest.match(/^(\\s*[&*][^\\s,[\\]{}]+)/);\n\t\t\tif (bind !== rest.slice(bind[1].length);\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tconst quote = rest.match(/^(\\s*\"([^\"\\\\]|\\\\.)*\"|\\s*'[^']*')/);\n\t\t\tif (quote !== null && quote[1] !== void 0) rest.match(/^[\\s,[\\]{}]+/);\n\t\t\tif (flow !== null) {\n\t\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttext: flow[0],\n\t\t\t\t\tcls: \"structure\"\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\trest = rest.slice(flow[0].length);\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tconst plain = rest.match(/^[^,[\\]{}]+?(?=\\s*(?:[,[\\]{}]|\\s#|$))/);\n\t\t\tif (plain !== null && plain[0].trim() !== \"\") {\n\t\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\t\ttext: plain[0],\n\t\t\t\t\tcls: \"scalar\"\n\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\trest = rest.slice(plain[0].length);\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tconst first = rest[0] ?? \"\";\n\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\ttext: first,\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"plain\"\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t\trest = rest.slice(first.length);\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn tokens;\n\t}\n\t/** Highlight one line; never throws and never loses characters. */\n\tfunction highlightLine(line) {\n\t\tconst indentMatch = line.match(/^[ \\t]*/);\n\t\tconst indent = indentMatch === null ? \"\" : indentMatch[0];\n\t\tlet rest = line.slice(indent.length);\n\t\tif (rest.trim() === \"\" || rest.trimStart().startsWith(\"#\")) return rest === \"\" ? {\n\t\t\tindent,\n\t\t\ttokens: []\n\t\t} : {\n\t\t\tindent,\n\t\t\ttokens: [{\n\t\t\t\ttext: rest,\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"comment\"\n\t\t\t}]\n\t\t};\n\t\tconst tokens = [];\n\t\twhile (true) {\n\t\t\tconst dash = rest.match(/^(-\\s+)/);\n\t\t\tif (dash === null || dash[1] === void 0) break;\n\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\ttext: dash[1],\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"structure\"\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t\trest = rest.slice(dash[1].length);\n\t\t}\n\t\tconst keyMatch = rest.match(/^(\"(?:[^\"\\\\]|\\\\.)*\"|'[^']*'|[^:#]+?)\\s*:(?=\\s|$)/);\n\t\tif (keyMatch !== null && keyMatch[1] !== void 0 && keyMatch[0] !== void 0) {\n\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\ttext: keyMatch[1],\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"key\"\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t\ttokens.push({\n\t\t\t\ttext: rest.slice(keyMatch[1].length, keyMatch[0].length),\n\t\t\t\tcls: \"structure\"\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t\trest = rest.slice(keyMatch[0].length);\n\t\t}\n\t\tconst commentAt = rest.indexOf(\" #\");\n\t\tlet value = rest;\n\t\tlet comment = \"\";\n\t\tif (commentAt >= 0) {\n\t\t\tvalue = rest.slice(0, commentAt + 1);\n\t\t\tcomment = rest.slice(commentAt + 1);\n\t\t}\n\t\tif (value.trim() !== \"\") tokens.push(...scalarTokens(value));\n\t\tif (comment !== \"\") tokens.push({\n\t\t\ttext: comment,\n\t\t\tcls: \"comment\"\n\t\t});\n\t\treturn {\n\t\t\tindent,\n\t\t\ttokens\n\t\t};\n\t}\n\t/** Highlight a whole document into lines. */\n\tfunction highlightYaml(text) {\n\t\treturn text.split(\"\\n\").map((line) => highlightLine(line));\n\t}\n\t/** A top-level mapping key line (unindented, not a comment or list item). */\n\tfunction isTopLevelKey(line) {\n\t\treturn /^[^\\s#-]/.test(line);\n\t}\n\t/** Whether every line collected so far is a comment or blank (a doc header). */\n\tfunction isCommentHeader(lines) {\n\t\treturn lines.every((line) => line.trim() === \"\" || line.trimStart().startsWith(\"#\"));\n\t}\n\t/**\n\t* Group document lines into foldable regions by top-level key. Leading\n\t* comment lines merge into the first key's region (they are its header in\n\t* wa-cli's artifacts), and indented, list, comment and blank lines attach to\n\t* the region above them, so every input line lands in exactly one region.\n\t*/\n\tfunction foldYaml(text) {\n\t\tconst folds = [];\n\t\tlet current = null;\n\t\tfor (const line of text.split(\"\\n\")) {\n\t\t\tif (current !== null && isTopLevelKey(line) && !isCommentHeader(current.lines)) current = null;\n\t\t\tif (current === null) {\n\t\t\t\tcurrent = {\n\t\t\t\t\theader: line,\n\t\t\t\t\tlines: [line]\n\t\t\t\t};\n\t\t\t\tfolds.push(current);\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcurrent.lines.push(line);\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn folds;\n\t}\n\t// src/queueview.ts\n\t/**\n\t* Pure view models for the two author action queues: the decision queue\n\t* (decisions.yaml) and the author-review records (author-reviews.yaml).\n\t*\n\t* The snapshot already carries both files as full text (see ./snapshot);\n\t* these parsers turn that text into display rows for the panel's queue\n\t* views. The same discipline as every other view module applies: tolerant\n\t* per-field extraction, never throw, no fetching, no React, no DOM. A\n\t* missing file (null text) is no data, an empty file is no data, and text\n\t* that fails to parse surfaces as a readable parseError the client shows\n\t* instead of an empty list pretending everything is decided.\n\t*/\n\tfunction isRecord$2(value) {\n\t\treturn typeof value === \"object\" && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);\n\t}\n\tfunction asString$1(value) {\n\t\treturn typeof value === \"string\" && value !== \"\" ? value : null;\n\t}\n\tfunction asStringArray(value) {\n\t\treturn Array.isArray(value) ? value.filter((item) => typeof item === \"string\" && item !== \"\") : [];\n\t}\n\t/** Parse YAML text; a missing/empty file is no data, a failure is an error. */\n\tfunction parseDoc(text) {\n\t\tif (typeof text !== \"string\") return {\n\t\t\tdoc: null,\n\t\t\terror: null,\n\t\t\tabsent: true\n\t\t};\n\t\tif (text.trim() === \"\") return {\n\t\t\tdoc: null,\n\t\t\terror: null,\n\t\t\tabsent: true\n\t\t};\n\t\ttry {\n\t\t\treturn {\n\t\t\t\tdoc: parse(text),\n\t\t\t\terror: null,\n\t\t\t\tabsent: false\n\t\t\t};\n\t\t} catch (cause) {\n\t\t\treturn {\n\t\t\t\tdoc: null,\n\t\t\t\terror: String(cause?.message ?? cause),\n\t\t\t\tabsent: false\n\t\t\t};\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t/** Parse decisions.yaml text into decision rows; never throws. */\n\tfunction decisionEntries(text) {\n\t\tconst { doc, error, absent } = parseDoc(text);\n\t\tif (error !== null) return {\n\t\t\tentries: [],\n\t\t\tparseError: error\n\t\t};\n\t\tif (absent || !isRecord$2(doc)) return {\n\t\t\tentries: [],\n\t\t\tparseError: null\n\t\t};\n\t\tconst raw = Array.isArray(doc.decisions) ? doc.decisions : [];\n\t\tconst entries = [];\n\t\tfor (const item of raw) {\n\t\t\tif (!isRecord$2(item)) continue;\n\t\t\tconst id = asString$1(item.id);\n\t\t\tif (id === null) continue;\n\t\t\tconst recommendation = asString$1(item.recommendation);\n\t\t\tconst options = Array.isArray(item.options) ? item.options.flatMap((option) => {\n\t\t\t\tif (!isRecord$2(option)) return [];\n\t\t\t\tconst optionId = asString$1(option.id);\n\t\t\t\tif (optionId === null) return [];\n\t\t\t\treturn [{\n\t\t\t\t\tid: optionId,\n\t\t\t\t\ttext: asString$1(option.text) ?? optionId,\n\t\t\t\t\trecommended: optionId === recommendation\n\t\t\t\t}];\n\t\t\t}) : [];\n\t\t\tentries.push({\n\t\t\t\tid,\n\t\t\t\tstatus: asString$1(item.status),\n\t\t\t\tquestion: asString$1(item.question),\n\t\t\t\toptions,\n\t\t\t\trecommendation,\n\t\t\t\trationale: asString$1(item.rationale),\n\t\t\t\tblocking: item.blocking === true,\n\t\t\t\tunits: asStringArray(item.units)\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn {\n\t\t\tentries,\n\t\t\tparseError: null\n\t\t};\n\t}\n\t/** Parse author-reviews.yaml text into review rows; never throws. */\n\tfunction reviewEntries(text) {\n\t\tconst { doc, error, absent } = parseDoc(text);\n\t\tif (error !== null) return {\n\t\t\tentries: [],\n\t\t\tparseError: error\n\t\t};\n\t\tif (absent || !isRecord$2(doc)) return {\n\t\t\tentries: [],\n\t\t\tparseError: null\n\t\t};\n\t\tconst raw = Array.isArray(doc.reviews) ? null,\n\t\t\tauthorReviews: snapshot?.authorReviews?.text ?? null\n\t\t};\n\t}\n\t// src/overview.ts\n\t/**\n\t* Pure view model for the overview dashboard and unit-tree navigation.\n\t*\n\t* Everything here derives display data from one snapshot route body and\n\t* nothing else: no fetching, no React, no DOM. The client bundle imports it\n\t* (tsdown inlines it, together with the yaml parser, into the iife), and the\n\t* tests drive it with real snapshots built from testdata.\n\t*\n\t* Structural state follows wa-cli: the unit tree and per-unit stage,\n\t* progress, blockers and commands come from the `status --json` rows the\n\t* snapshot embeds. When that area is degraded the tree falls back to the\n\t* hierarchy recorded in work.yaml, and finally to the directory-owning units\n\t* the snapshot walked, so the panel always has something to navigate.\n\t* Hand-edited YAML that does not parse never throws — it simply yields no\n\t* data for the field it would have supplied.\n\t*/\n\t/** Command suggested when a workspace has no works yet. */\n\tconst NEW_WORK_COMMAND = \"wa-cli new <genre> <id>\";\n\t/** How many blocker messages a work card shows. */\n\tconst BLOCKER_SUMMARY_LIMIT = 2;\n\tfunction isRecord$1(value) {\n\t\treturn
